## Document 025: 2026-10-27_［門間一夫の経済深読み］成長戦略より21世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」を_218ba051

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-10-27_［門間一夫の経済深読み］成長戦略より21世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」を_218ba051.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

門間 一夫の 
経済深読み

成長戦略より21 世紀ニッポンの「 ア
ポロ計画」を 
みずほ総合研究所  
エグゼクティブエコノミスト  門間一夫 202 6 年 10 月 27 日

心情的にはノーサレンダー路線 
先月の論考では、①度重なる成長戦略にもかかわらず日本の潜在成長率は低下したこと、②成長戦略
には政策手段の特定が困難という基本問題があるこ と 、③25 年英知を尽くしても結果が出なかった事実
は厳粛に受け止めるべきこと、などを述べた 1。 
それを踏まえ、本稿ではあるべき方向性を考えてみたい。選択肢は、(a)25 年で諦めず50 年でも100
年でもやる、(b)成長目標を達成しやすい水準に下げる、(c)政府のミッションは成長戦略とは別にある
と考える、の 3 つである。選択肢(b)が無難に見えるが、もともと成長戦略は「現状の成長率ではだめ
だ」という認識に基づくものである。現状を明確に上回る成長率を目指さない限り、成長戦略としては
自己矛盾である。 
すると残るのは (a)ノーサレンダー路線、(c)ミッション再考、 のいずれかである。このうちノーサレ
ンダー路線、つまり「成長を諦めない」という不屈の姿勢には人々の共感が得られやすい。しかし、不
屈の志を掲げても、手段の裏付けを欠くという現実は変わらない。手段の裏付けを欠くなら「目標」で
はなく「夢」である。もちろん、夢を語ることが悪いわけではないし、政治家は夢を語るべきという考
えもあるだろう。その夢が偶然にでもかなえば歴史に残る偉業であり、夢なら実現せずとも落胆の度合
いは小さい。ノーサレンダー路線はアップサイドだけでダウンサイドはない、と考えてよいだろうか。 
成長戦略のダウンサイド 
成長戦略にダウンサイドはない、やってみて損はない、と い う前提は実は正しくない。成長戦略は「た
だ」でできるわけではないからだ。例えば、成長戦略には多かれ少なかれネット財政支出が伴う。国債
をどれだけ発行できるのか議論の余地はあるが、いずれにせよ有限である。 
一方、成長戦略は総花的になりやすい。成長を実現する手段がはっきりしない以上、ある政策案を「成
長とは無関係」ときっぱり排除するのも難しいからである。経済界の要望や政治家の信念に基づく様々
な政策案が、成長に少しでも関係しそうなら幅広く正当化される。各種施策の費用対効果について一定

1 門間一夫の経済深読み「成長戦略の本質的な限界」 （2026年 9 月25 日 ）。

### Page 2

2

の分析や議論は行われている。しかし、行われることとそれが有効に機能することは別である。費用対
効果の分析が有効に機能し、各種施策の経済成長へのインパクトが正確に測れていたとしたら、25 年に
わたり成長目標の大幅未達という結果にはならなかったはずである。 
一般に、政策目標の実現に向けてリソースが投入され、目標が実現できなかった場合、そのリソース
投入は失敗だったことになる。チャレンジ自体は無駄ではなかったと言える場合もあるが、過去 25 年
だめだった目標にもう一度挑戦してみるという話なら、たぶん次もだめだろうと事前に予想できる。金
も人もふんだんにあるなら別だが、財政状況が厳しく、人材の不足も叫ばれる国で、高確率で失敗する
ミッションへのリソース投入は賢明な選択と言えるのか。 
有限のリソースが無駄に使われれば、他の課題のために使えるリソースが減る。ここで言うリソース
は財政資金だけではない。実現できない目的のために、多忙な官僚の労力が割かれ、政治家のポリティ
カル ・ キャピタルも消耗すれば、やればできたはずの他の課題への対応まで中途半端に終わってしまう。
成長戦略には他の課題を犠牲にするという「機会費用」が伴うのであり、それが「ただではない」とい
うことのより正確な意味である。「成長を諦めないノーサレンダー路線でいく」という選択は、「50 年で
も 100 年でもその機会費用を払い続けることを覚悟する」という選択でもある。 
何のための経済成長か 
ここで話を一段階前に戻してみよう。そもそもなぜ、経済成長を高めるべきと考えられているのだろ
うか。もちろん、低成長より高成長の方がいいに決まっているので、経済成長を簡単に高められるので
あれば、そんな疑問を持つ必要はない。しかし、経済成長を高めようとすることで機会費用を払い続け
ることになる確率が高いなら、そうまでしてわれわれが最終的に望んでいるものは何なのかという問い
に、きちんと向き合う必要がある。 
経済成長の必要性を訴える主張は、「〇〇の解決には経済成長が不可欠」「低成長のままなら〇〇のよ
うな不都合が起きる」というパターンにおおむね当てはまるように思う。つまり、現在直面している課
題、あるいは将来直面しそうな課題に対応するには、より高い経済成長が必要という認識が成長戦略の
基礎にあると考えられる。念頭に置かれている課題の中身は、社会保障や財政の持続性、行政・医療・
介護サービスの維持、国際的な存在感の回復、生活水準の向上など、論者により様々である。パイを増
やせるなら改善できる課題は多いので、経済成長を高めるべきという主張は万人受けし、政治家には格
好の政策アジェンダになる。 
しかし、「何らかの課題を経済成長で解決する」という考え方は 、「その課題に直接向き合うより経済
成長を高める方が簡単だ」という前提がないと成立しない。その前提が間違っていれば、むしろ経済成
長に頼るがゆえに肝心の課題がいつまでも解決しないことになる。重要な課題であればあるほど、経済
成長と切り離して解決する努力を早く始めるべきではないか。 
例えば、社会保障や財政の持続性が課題なのであれば、給付と負担のバランスや、恵まれている人と
そうでない人の公平感を改善することで、問題の解決を目指す方が良い。人々の生活水準を高めたいと
いう課題を経済成長と切り離すのは少し厄介だが、国民全員が等しく困っているわけではなく、問題の

### Page 3

3

本質は経済格差にある。分配政策により、国民生活の満足感や社会の安定性を全体として改善すること
はできる。 
国際社会における日本の存在感を高めることは、経済成長ではそもそも無理であろう。かつて日本が
世界第2 位のGDP をキープし、経済大国として存在感を示した時代もあった。しかし、あれは冷戦構造
のもとでの日米関係、戦後の高度成長とその余熱、新興国が発展初期段階にあったことなどによる歴史
の偶然であった。GDP の対世界シェアが3～4％程度まで落ち、今後さらに人口が減り、資源・エネルギ
ーに乏しく、特殊な言語を使う島国が、今から経済成長を多少高められたとしても、そのことで示せる
国際パワーは高が知れている。小さくても存在感がある国をどう作るかが、正しいアジェンダ設定だと
思われる。 
21 世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」は？ 
日本が課題だらけなのは確かだが、25 年間どうやっても達成できなかった経済成長に、その解決をこ
れ以上頼るのが正解とは思いにくい。逆に、優先課題の解決に国が本気でリソースを投入するなら、そ
こに民間も収益機会を見出し、様々な連鎖反応を通じて経済に好影響が及ぶ可能性も出てくる。 
経済学者マリアナ・マッツカートは著書「ミッション・エコノミー」において、大きな変革は市場メ
カニズムだけでは生み出せず、国家が野心的だが実行可能な課題を設定し、民間の力を引き出す仕組み
を築くことが重要と説く 2。マッツカートは代表例として、1960 年代の米国アポロ計画を挙げる。1969
年 7 月 20 日、 ニール・アームストロングとバズ・オルドリンが月面に降り立ったことで知られる、あの
アポロ計画である。 
日本の成長戦略も「野心的な課題」を掲げたものではあるが、複雑すぎるマクロ経済そのものを課題
の対象にすると、先月の論考で詳しく述べたように、目的と手段の関係性が特定できない。原因と結果
の関係がよくわからない仕事は、責任の所在があいまいで互いに人任せになり、結果にコミットするこ
との効果が得られない。それに対し、「月に人を送る」などのより具体的なミッションの場合、そのため
には何が必要なのかの因果関係を分析・認識しやすい分、結果にコミットすれば人々の力が正しい方向
へ向かう。 
アポロ計画は、半導体、コンピューター、通信、材料工学などの技術を飛躍的に発展させ、その後の
米国の産業基盤を築いたと言われている。また、計画推進の国家機関であった米航空宇宙局 （NASA）は 、
多くの科学技術人材を育成・輩出した。彼らはその後、金融イノベーションなどでも活躍し、NASA 出身
なので「ロケット・サイエンティスト」と呼ばれたことはよく知られている。 
ここで重要なのは、半導体やコンピューターなどの産業育成自体を目的として、政府が補助金で支援
したという話ではないことである。アポロ計画という具体的なミッションがまず存在し、それへの貢献
に適正な対価で報いる仕組みがイノベーターたちを突き動かし、様々な技術や知見が開発、蓄積された
のである。後世に残った産業基盤や人材は、「月に人を送る」というミッションの副産物であった。

2 Mazzucato, Mariana(2021) Mission Economy: A Moonshot Guide to Changing Capitalism, Allen Lane-Penguin 
（関美和・鈴木絵里子訳『ミッション・エコノミー』News Picks、2021 年 ）。

### Page 4

4

一般論として、何らかの課題を解決すれば、経済に必ず大きなプラスの効果が見込める、とまでは言
えない。しかし、上位目標が「課題の解決」なのだから、それが達成された時点でミッションは成功で
あり、投入されたリソースは「有効に使われた」ことになる。そのうえで、マクロ経済にもプラスの波
及効果があればなお良いが、仮にそれがなくても課題が解決された分、以前より望ましい社会になって
いる。 
最大の難関は、優先課題を特定しそれに取り組む社会的モメンタムを生み出すことであり、それには
既存の発想に捉われない強力なビジョンとリーダーシップが要る。アポロ計画にも当初「無謀で財政資
金の無駄使い」という声があった。マッツカートは先ほどの著書の中で、仮に現代風の費用対効果分析
を適用していたら、アポロ計画は採用されず、人類が月面に立つことはなかったと言っている。 
アポロ計画に相当する案件は、どの時代、どの国にも自明なものとしてあるわけではない。 「月に人を
送る」というミッションは、当時の米ソ冷戦状況において、ソ連に宇宙開発で打ち勝つことが米国の死
活問題、という歴史特殊的な認識のもとで生まれた。それでも、よく考えてみれば今の日本にも、人口
の減少 ・ 高齢化や地政学環境の激変など、 人類史上にもまれな歴史特殊的な条件のもとで、 「死活問題」
になりつつある課題はいくつかあるように思う。例えば、エネルギーや食料の安定供給、生活インフラ ・
医療・介護体制の維持・改善などは、次世代の日本人にとってまさに「死活問題」なのではないか。 
限られたリソースの優先課題への配分が、市場メカニズムで実現できないなら、国が強力に関与しな
ければならない。民間には不得意な国民的課題の解決こそ、国の本来の役割であり、そのためのリソー
スを国債発行でまかないきれないなら、国民に追加負担を求めてもいい。 
言うまでもなくこれは困難な作業である。だから優先課題は何か、そのために国民負担を求めるべき
かなどを深く議論せず、経済成長で様々な問題をまとめて解決できることにしておく方が、実はずっと
楽なのである。しかし、それが問題の先送りにすぎないことは繰り返し述べたとおりである。今も成長
戦略が重要だと言い、政府にそれを求めたがるわれわれの意識は、日本が直面する真の課題やその優先
順位を考えるのは面倒だ、 という思考停止から生まれているのかもしれない。21 世紀ニッポンの「アポ
ロ計画」は何なのか、国民的な議論の活性化が必要ではないだろうか。

## Document 113: 261103_daiwa_sunon

Source PDF: all report pdf/261103_daiwa_sunon.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Industrials

What’s new: We provide a 3Q26 results preview on Sunon, which plans to 
release its 3Q26 results on 6 November. We reaffirm our Buy (1) call as we 
expect the 4Q26 and 2027 outlook for its telecom, servers, industrial and 
auto segments to remain intact with both gross and operating margin set to 
expand. We now forecast a net profit CAGR of 28.2% over 2026-28E from 
21.2% previously (see our flash, 28 August).

What’s the impact: 3Q26 results preview. Sunon’s 3Q26 revenue of 
TWD6,143m (+13.4% QoQ and +24.5% YoY), a record-high level, 
accounted for 97.2% and 102.2%, respectively, of our previous 
(TWD6,319m, +16.6% QoQ and +28.1% YoY) and the Bloomberg 
consensus estimates for 3Q26 revenue. We forecast its 3Q26 gross margin 
to be 32.8% (vs. 31.3% in 2Q26 and 31.3% in 3Q25), slightly higher than 
the Bloomberg consensus estimate of 31.9%. We forecast an operating 
profit of TWD1,115m (+33.3% QoQ and +45.0% YoY), 3.0% higher than 
the Bloomberg consensus estimate of TWD1,082m and an operating 
margin of 18.1% (vs. 15.4% in 2Q26 and 15.6% in 3Q25). Overall, we 
forecast a net profit of TWD901m (+30.5% QoQ, +29.7% YoY), 1.9% 
higher than the consensus estimate of TWD884m. We forecast 4Q26 
revenue to grow 7.5% QoQ, and 1Q27 revenue to grow 1.4% QoQ 
considering its ASIC and GPU projects.

Rising liquid cooling revenue and EC fan exposure in 2027E underpin 
its gross margin expansion. We expect Sunon’s cold plate products (ie, 
enterprise and GPU clients), pump and CDU to be the key revenue drivers 
with certification also underway for CSP clients. We expect revenue from 
EC fans to reach 10% in 2027E for the industrial segment. We revise up 
our 2026-28E EPS by 1-13%, mainly on higher revenue, gross margin and 
operating margin assumptions.

What we recommend: We reaffirm our Buy (1) call and raise our 12M TP 
to TWD265 (from TWD236), still based on a target PER of 18x vs. its past-
5-year PER range of 6-25x, on the 2027E EPS, given solid earnings 
visibility from intact demand in the server, telecom, industrial and auto 
segments. We believe its dividend yield of 5.6-9.2% over 2026-28E will 
offer downside support for its share price. Downside risks: worse-than-
expected gross-margin expansion and AI server development progress.

How we differ: We are one of only two overseas brokers covering the 
stock. Our 2026-28E EPS are 4-12% above the Bloomberg consensus, 
likely due to our more aggressive revenue, gross margin and operating 
margin estimates.

3 November 2026

3Q26 results preview: ongoing gross margin expansion

➢ Rising liquid cooling and EC fans revenue mix in 2027E vs. 2026 
➢ Likely upward revision in EPS forecasts in the near term 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) call; raising 12M TP to TWD265

Company profile 
Founded in 1980 and listed on Taiwan’s main board in 2000, Sunonwealth Electric Machine Industry 
(Sunon) is one of the top-4 fan and motor providers in the world, along with Delta Electronics, Nidec and 
ebm-papst. Its major clients are Apple, Dell, Lenovo, Bosch, Siemens and Tesla. Its applications include 
IT & OE, telecoms & servers, industrial & medical equipment, home applications, distribution, autos and 
LED lighting.

### Page 5

5

Sunonwealth Electric Machine Industry (2421 TT): 3 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 8 May 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
The CEO and chairperson of Sunonwealth are the same individual; Sunonwealth's board is led
by the Hung family. We see no issues with the company’s board. We view the chairman leading
the R&D team as a sign of the company’s continuing focus on developing new products to
achieve a long-term competitive advantage.
Capital management 2 Sunonwealth's dividend payout ratio was 83-88% in 2015-18, but it decreased to 60-69% during 2022-25 due to its capacity expansion plans.
Related party &
transaction 2 Revenue generated from related-party transactions was less than 1% of total revenue during 2023-25.
E Materials sourcing & efficiency 1
Sunonwealth’s sourcing of raw materials has been relatively stable (including IC, copper, plastic,
aluminium and steel), and we also note that Sunonwealth abides by RoHS regulations and
relevant international standards (ISO14001, IECQ QC080000, ISO9001) for its material sourcing
activities. With the ongoing ESG trend, its clients may move towards a more energy-efficient
production cycle, which will likely benefit Sunonwealth’s energy-efficient products.
E Product design & lifecycle management 2
Sunonwealth has improved its product designs to stronger but lighter form factors. Besides, we
have observed more green projects utilising Sunonwealth's capabilities. Thus, we believe
Sunonwealth has strong product design and lifecycle management capabilities to address the
ongoing changing business environment.
S Product quality & safety 2 Sunonwealth has its own Quality Strategy Centre to evaluate quality issues. With international standards in place, we believe Sunonwealth has taken relevant measures to address product quality and safety issues.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Grand Process Technology Corp. (3131.TWO): SoIC / PLP to support 
future GM enhancement; 3Q26 GM miss
3 November 2026 | 10:54PM HKT
GPTC reported its 3Q26 preliminary results. Revenues was pre-announced, while GM 
came in at 36.6% (down 2.3ppt QoQ; down 4.2ppt YoY), below GSe/cons. of 
39.7%/40.6%, which we attribute to an unfavorable product mix. Opex ratio further 
improved to 14.6% (vs. 17.7% in 2Q26), better than our / Bloomberg consensus 
estimates of 15.1% / 16.3%, leading OP income to +34% QoQ, or +54% YoY. Overall, 
the miss on GM leads EPS miss our / Bloomberg consensus by 9% / 14%. Despite 
3Q26 GM miss, we believe GPTC’s multi-year growth outlook remains intact, with 
continuous capacity increase to better capture the demand from CoWoS capacity 
expansion, along with product mix upgrade toward SoIC and PLP , carrying higher 
content value. Maintain Buy.

Earnings revision: We factor in 3Q26 results and cut EPS by 4% / 2% / 0% in 
2026-28E, mainly on lower GM, as we slowdown the pace of product mix upgrade 
toward SoIC and PLP . Despite the cut, we continue to expect GM sequential 
enhancement, with 4Q26 GM up to 37.7% (vs. 36.6% in 3Q26) and up to 40.2% / 
42.5% in 2027-28E, driven by rising SoIC and PLP contribution.

and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

King Yuan Electronics Co (2449.TW)

Near-term weakness already in the price; maintain Buy and lower TP to NT$286
- Despite near-term margin pressure from softer-than-expected quarterly revenue
and rising depreciation cost, electricity and OPEX, management sees GM
expansion into next year supported by stronger utilization, operating leverage, and
favorable AI-related product mix, in particular substantial upside from wafer

sorting. We lower our earnings projection for 2026/2027 /2028 by 6%/17%/15%
reflecting near-term GM shortfall and potential increasing depreciation cost.
Considering remaining strong AI cycle, we keep our Buy rating but lower our DCF-
derived TP to NT$286 given our lower estimates and concerns on recent
macro/policy uncertainty. Our new TP of NT$286 implies 25x/17x of our 2027 /28
EPS estimates.

### Page 6

King Yuan Electronics Co (2449.TW)
06 November 2026 Citi Research
6
King Yuan Electronics Co
Company description
Founded in 1987, King Yuan Electronics Corp. (KYEC) mainly provides testing
services for the back-end process of semiconductor production globally. With
more than 30 years of industry experience, KYEC has built up firm
relationships with several leading global IC design/IDM customers. Its
headquarters and production facilities are both located in Taiwan, with sales

offices in North America, Japan, and Singapore.
Investment strategy
We rate KYEC as Buy. As a key testing service provider for AI GPU and ASIC
products, KYEC is well positioned to benefit from the significant AI growth
opportunity in the years to come. Currently, KYEC is the major final testing
service provider for its major customer's AI GPU, and we expect KYEC to
continue to secure its dominant share in its customer's upcoming AI GPU
product lines due to its specialty in burn-in testing, smooth product delivery

capability, and its strong business partnership with its customer. We are also
positive over the growth outlook of its smartphone business thanks to the
integration of AI functionality, leading to a better replacement cycle. We
believe KYEC's major customer in smartphone will benefit from an improved
product mix, which will result in longer testing times for the flagship SoC.
Valuation
Our target price for KYEC of NT$286 is derived using a DCF methodology.

Using Bloomberg data, we apply a risk-free rate of 4%, a risk premium of 5%,
and a beta of 1.4x for a 10.8% cost of equity. With 20% corporate tax and a
3.0% interest rate on its debt, our WACC for KYEC is 9.7%. We assume
terminal sales growth of 5%. We model its adjusted EBIT margin to gradually
improve from 23% in 2024 to c30% in 2035E. At our target price, the shares
would trade at 2027 /28E P/B of 4x/4x and 2027 /28E P/E of 25x/17x, in line
with its global peers.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent KYEC shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected end-demand for global
smartphone/consumer electronic products; 2) slower ramp for AI-related
products; 3) slower-than-expected demand recovery in the automotive and
base station sector; and 4) pricing pressure on increasing competition.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific

In this report, we focus on Delta's monthly sales, which we

cooling products.

and 34% of consensus at NT$219,806mn (+12% QoQ/+36% YoY).

• We think 4Q26 revenue is tracking slightly ahead of market expectations.
• We think the slightly better Oct monthly sales should help on near-term
sentiment, though more meaningful upside to market expectations on
earnings growth might  depend on the penetration of HVDC power racks into
the coming years.
• We continue to see Delta as a key beneficiary of the increasing power
consumption of AI clusters through its comprehensive power/cooling

offerings. Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28e EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 59% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

個股報告 
富世達(6805) 
伺服器占比突破八成，QD量價齊揚推升獲利 
投資評等 目標價 
買進 $3000 
前次投資建議 2026.08.05: 
「買進」 ，目標價: $2360

報告日期：2026.11.04 研 究 員：陳威仲

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 26Q3 毛利率優於預期，伺服器產品營收占比升至 81.3%，上修 2026/27 年 EPS 至
65.5/120.1 元(前次預估 64.3/112.8元)， 目標價由2360 元調升至 3000 元(25X 27(F)PER)，
維持【買進】投資評等。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/3 收盤價： 2160.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 45.0 億元 (QoQ+20.4%/YoY+26.2%)，受
VR200、ASIC 平台小量拉貨與非奇鋐之快接頭客戶出貨
增加帶動；毛利率 38.0%(季增 3.9ppts/年增 10.3ppts，
優於預期 2.9ppts)，主因伺服器產品營收占比升至
81.3%；營業淨利 14.6 億元(QoQ+39.9%/YoY+84.2%)；
稅後淨利 12.4億元(QoQ+46.3%/YoY+80.8%)，EPS 18.1
元。 
➢ QD 受惠 GPU 與 ASIC 雙平台放量及規格升級，Rubin 
Ultra 將導入單價約兩倍的 ZQD，AWS Trn3 等 ASIC 專
案將於 2027 年陸續貢獻；AI 滑軌亦陸續通過驗證，27
年可望隨 VR200與 ASIC專案放量。 
➢ 華為摺疊機新品拉貨遞延，加上記憶體漲價影響終端需
求 ， 下 修26Q4 營 收 預 估 至54.0 億元
(QoQ+20.0%/YoY+35.8%)；惟伺服器產品比重上升，毛
利率上修至 38.1%，EPS 21.63元。 
➢ 預估 2026/27 年 EPS 65.5/120.1 元，將持續受惠強勁的
伺服器需求與水冷滲透率提升之下帶動獲利成長，目前股
價分別位於 2026/27 年 EPS的 33.0/18.0X，過去股價區
間落在 15~87X，目標價 3000 元(25X 27(F)PER)，維持
【買進】投資評等，潛在上漲空間 38.9%。

營 運 分 析

富世達為 AI基礎設施與高階摺疊機轉軸的關鍵供應商 
富世達股份有限公司成立於民國 2001 年，為奇鋐(3017)持股 16.87%的轉投
資公司，總部與研發中心位於新北新莊，並於越南、中國深圳設有生產基地，
提供各類金屬沖壓設計與製造，主要產品為伺服器組件(滑軌、水冷快接頭)，
與摺疊手機、筆記型電腦、平板電腦之轉軸零件，從消費性電子零組件廠轉
型為 AI基礎設施與高階摺疊機轉軸的關鍵供應商，主要出貨終端包含華為、
Motorola 等摺疊機，與各大AI伺服器平台(NVIDIA、AMD、ASIC等)機櫃用
的滑軌與水冷快接頭。

26Q3 伺服器產品營收占比上升至 81.3%，帶動毛利率大幅優於預期。 
富世達 26Q3 營收 45.0 億元(QoQ+20.4%/YoY+26.2%，優於兆豐前次預期
4%)，因 VR200、ASIC 平台小量拉貨與非奇鋐之快接頭客戶規模上升使伺
服器快接頭營收增長強勁；毛利率 38.0%(季增 3.9ppts/年增 10.3ppts，優於
兆豐預期 2.9ppts)，主因產品組合改善，伺服器產品營收占比超越 80%；營
業淨利 14.6 億元 (QoQ+39.9%/YoY+84.2%)， 稅 後 淨 利12.4 億元
(QoQ+46.3%/YoY+80.8%)，EPS 18.1元(前次預估 15.9 元)。

資料來源：Cmoney、兆豐投顧整理與預估

QD 受惠 GPU與 ASIC雙平台放量及規格升級，為伺服器產品主要成長動能 
26Q3 富世達伺服器產品占營收 81.3%，其中快接頭(QD)約 90%，整體單季
稼動率約 80~90%；而目前新產能開出後 QD 產能已達 1350 萬顆/季。今年
QD 營收貢獻以 GPU 平台為主，明年 ASIC 比重將持續提高，預期 2028 年
ASIC 比重有望超越 GPU；其中(1)AWS Trn3 已配合客戶時程出貨，將於
27H1顯著貢獻營收。(2)Google TPU V9世代有望由現行的 NSP1接頭，變
為導入混用 QD+NSP1接頭，以優化未來光模塊、DIMM 等部件潛在導入水
冷系統後維護的容易性。(3)Meta MTIA 與微軟 MAIA 等 AISC 專案也將於
27H2 出貨帶動 QD 市場規模提升。而在 Rubin Ultra 世代，將導入 ZQD 於
運算托盤(取代部份的 UQD)與 rack manifold之快接頭，且ZQD單顆價值預
估將為現行 MQD與 UQD 單價的兩倍，ZQD 預計 12月進入量產準備。 
規格面，VR200 單櫃 QD 數量約 538 顆(含 rack manifold 88 顆)，Rubin 
Ultra(VR300)將為 574~664顆，預估 QD量價齊揚趨勢延續，為2027~2028
年主要成長動能。

AI 滑軌陸續通過驗證，27年可望隨 VR200與 ASIC 專案量產放量 
滑軌事業目前月產能維持 35 萬套，目前以通用型為主，稼動率約 3 成；隨
明年 AI專案的量產，稼動率可望提升至50~60%以上，毛利率也將隨著 ASIC
水冷專案的放量同步改善，水冷櫃滑軌對承重、緩衝與平行度要求較高，ASP
亦較高，公司預期明年滑軌維持高度成長，後續將視訂單規劃擴廠。

### Page 4

評等分級：買進（預期未來六個月股價漲幅超過+30%）; 逢低買進（預期未來六個月股價漲幅介於+15%與+30%之間）; 區間操
作（預期未來六個月股價漲跌幅介於-15%與+15%之間）; 逢高賣出（預期未來六個月股價跌幅介於-15%與-30%之間）；賣出（預
期未來六個月股價跌幅超過-30%）。

投資之需求與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任。任何轉載或引用本報告內容必須經本公司同意。

- 4 - 
個股報告

華為摺疊機新品拉貨遞延與記憶體推升價格，轉軸營收短期承壓 
華為新機種受記憶體影響拉貨延後，加上終端需求轉弱，26Q3富世達手機
軸承營收占比降至 15.5%，預期26Q4手機轉軸拉貨動能有限。而華為 26H1
中國摺疊機市占逾 7成，摺疊機累計出貨突破 2,000 萬支；惟記憶體漲價使
單機平均成本增加約 200 美元，9/1 起主力機型最高調漲 17%，後續產品將
朝輕薄化發展並導入新材料，每代價格將重新議價，下一代機種仍由富世達
主供。雖蘋果 iPhone Duo 上市帶動摺疊機話題，將使摺疊機長期接受度與
滲透率同步提高，但短期消費性電子仍受記憶體價格推升單價而影響終端購
買意願。

26Q4 營收下修、毛利率上修，獲利預估小幅調降 
預估富世達 26Q4 營收 54.0 億元(QoQ+20.0%/YoY+35.8%，較原估下修
8.2%，主因摺疊機軸承拉貨不如預期 )；毛利率 38.1%(季增 0.1ppts/年增
9.0ppts，較原估上修 1.1ppts，主因 QD 生產進入規模化，帶動高毛利伺服
器比重上升)；稅後淨利 14.8 億元(QoQ+19.4%/YoY+97.3%，較原估下修
4.7%)，EPS 21.63元(原估 22.7 元)。

## Document 198: 8299群聯 兆豐

### Page 1

評等分級：買進（預期未來六個月股價漲幅超過+30%）; 逢低買進（預期未來六個月股價漲幅介於+15%與+30%之間）; 區間操
作（預期未來六個月股價漲跌幅介於-15%與+15%之間）; 逢高賣出（預期未來六個月股價跌幅介於-15%與-30%之間）；賣出（預
期未來六個月股價跌幅超過-30%）。 
本刊所刊載之內容僅做為參考，惟已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量

- 1 - 
個股報告 
群聯(8299) 
AI 需求未減，營收規模持續擴大 
投資評等 目標價 
買進 $2150 
前次投資建議：2026.08.14： 
「買進」 ，目標價: $4000

報告日期：2026.11.09 研 究 員：王穎

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 看好 AI及企業級相關產品需求維持強勁，且AI 產品組合佔比將進一步擴大，帶動營收規模
擴大，維持「買進」投資評等，然低價庫存利益將逐季下滑導致毛利率下降，因此下修目
標價至 2150 元(4 x 27(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 43.3%。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/6 收盤價： 1,500.00 ➢ 26Q3 營收 860.76 億元，QoQ+26.8%，YoY+374.6%，
毛利率 59.9%，費用 184.89 億元，QoQ+5.25 億元，業
外獲利 30.77 億元，稅後淨利305.46 億元，EPS 138 元。
受惠產品組合轉向企業級及 AI 應用，使營收規模擴大，
然手機、PC 等消費性需求受到 NAND 漲價下出貨量下
滑，但群聯本身 controller 出貨量及營收仍提升，主要因
市占率提升，同時低價庫存利益將減少導致毛利率下滑。 
➢ 展望 26Q4 預期 NAND Falsh 報價持續提升且企業級及
AI相關之產品需求維持強勁，預期AI Ecosystem module
將持續提升，預期營收續創新高，然庫存成本提高使毛利
率下滑。因此兆豐預估營收 991.6 億元，QoQ+15.2%，
YoY+334.9%，毛利率 56.4%，稅後淨利 308.22 億元，
EPS為 139.3元。 
➢ 展望 2026~2027 年，預期至 2027 仍呈現供不應求之態
勢，公司坐擁大量庫存， 且群聯產品組合轉向 AI

營 運 分 析

群聯為企業級 SSD領導廠商 
群聯電子股份有限公司成立於 2000 年 11月 8 日，為國內NAND FLASH控
制 IC 廠，主要業務為 NAND FLASH 控制 IC及周邊系統產品的研發設計製
造及銷售。公司已經成為USB隨身碟、SD記憶卡、eMMC、UFS、PATA、
SATA 與 PCIe 固態磁碟等控制晶片領域的領頭廠。 26Q3 產品組合 AI 
Ecosystem Module 57%、 Embedded ODM Module  24%、 Industrial 
Module9%、Controller 5%、Retail Module2%、Others 3%。

- 3 - 
個股報告 
26Q3產品組合轉佳帶動營收會模擴大 
26Q3 營收 860.76 億元，QoQ+26.8%，YoY+374.6%，毛利率 59.9%，費
用 184.89 億元，QoQ+5.25 億元，業外獲利 30.77 億元，稅後淨利 305.46
億元，EPS 138 元。受惠產品組合轉向企業級及AI應用，使營收規模擴大，
然手機、 PC 等消費性需求受到 NAND 漲價下出貨量下滑，但群聯本身
controller 出貨量及營收仍提升，主要因市占率提升，預期待消費性及工業市
場歷經庫存調整後，有望逐步恢復。同時低價庫存利益將減少導致毛利率下
滑。26Q3 庫存金額 1117.7億元，較 26Q2 增加 199.8 億元，存貨週轉天數
由 297天下降至 274 天，存貨主要配置於企業級、AI應用及 boot drive 等應
用，以因應後續客戶需求。

26Q4預期營收規模擴大獲利持續創高 
展望 26Q4預期 NAND Falsh報價持續提升，於 main drive持續取得不同客
戶 design win，main drive 對於 NAND容量需求更大，於 26Q3 取得多項新
專案 ， 預期26Q4~27Q1將持續增加 。 同時PCIe Gen6企業級 SSD預期 2026
年 11月底開始提供樣品，與 CPU 業者共同發布相關 solution，主要鎖定 AI
推論相關應用，因此企業級及 AI相關之產品需求將於 2027 年維持強勁，預
期 AI Ecosystem module 將持續提升，預期營收續創新高，然庫存成本提高
使毛利率下滑。因此兆豐預估營收991.6 億元，QoQ+15.2%，YoY+334.9%，
毛利率 56.4%，稅後淨利 308.22 億元，EPS為 139.3元。

- 4 - 
個股報告 
股利配發創下新高 
26H1 EPS達 187.4 元，董事會決議通過上半年度盈餘分配現金股利案，每
股擬配發 60 元現金股利，創下歷史新高，配發率約 32%，半年殖利率約
2.63%。若 26H2獲利及現金流更佳，2027 年 1月可再評估提高配發比例，
全年殖利率目標有望達 5%。 
圖三、NAND歷年供需比

資料來源：兆豐投顧整理

2026 NAND Flash供需缺口逐步擴大 
NAND FLASH 市場呈現明顯的週期性循環，每約 3.5～4年進入一個完整供
需週期，2026 NAND Flash 呈現供不應求，且下半年供需缺口擴大，有利報
價穩定甚至持續上漲。 同時 Nvidia ICMS (Inference Context Memory 
Storage) 的快取層級架構，用於解決大型語言模型推論時的記憶體瓶頸，推
升 NAND需求 ， 提升Token吞吐量 5 倍 、 推論延遲顯著下降 、 能效優化5 倍。
預期 2027 年用於 Nvidia KV Cache之 NAND 需求將達總需求約 10%。進一
步造成供需缺口擴大。

- 5 - 
個股報告 
預期 AI佔比提升帶動營收規模放大 
2026~2027 年，預期至 2027 仍呈現供不應求之態勢，公司坐擁大量庫存，
main drive、PCIe Gen6 等產品預期至 2027 年都將持續成長使群聯產品組合
轉向 AI Ecosystem module 有助於有利營收規模持續放大，另外在消費性市
場持續耕耘，取得更高手機、PC市占率，Client SSD方面，2026 年已取得
6 個 PCIe Gen5 TLC 及 QlC controller 專案，未來將協助原廠開發相容
aiDAPTIV之 Client SSD方案，結合 AI PC於記憶體擴充需求，擴大 PC市
場影響力，因此出貨量將持續增加，待消費性庫存歷經調整後，出貨量將進
一步提升，然隨著庫存成本持續提升，預期毛利率將下滑。因此兆豐預估營
收 2940.9/4442.2 億元，YoY+304.7/51.1%，毛利率 60.1/51.7%，稅後淨利
1027.6/1187.7億元，YoY+1075.8/15.6%，EPS為 464.7/536.9 元。

看好 AI相關產品組合佔比提升，維持「買進」投資評等 
群聯目前位於 2.8 X 27(F)PER)，看好 AI及企業級相關產品需求維持強勁，
且 AI產品組合佔比將進一步擴大，維持「買進」投資評等，然低價庫存利益
將逐季下滑導致毛利率下降，因此下修目標價至 2150 元(4 x 27(F)PER)，潛
在上漲空間 43.3%。

## Document 230: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다삼성전자(005930) 실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다실적은 좋지만, 주주환원은 아쉽다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
아직 수요의 끝은 보이지 않는다 
AI 투자에 대한 우려의 목소리도 있지만 정작 부품을 공급하고 투자하는 쪽의 목소
리는 시간이 갈수록 더 커지고 있다. 공급 부족의 시점 매 분기 뒤로 밀려나고 있기 
때문이다. 28년까지 공급부족이라고 했던 메모리업체들은 이구동성으로 30년까지 공
급부족이 지속된다고 언급하고 있다. 이러한 변화는 실적에 고스란히 반영되고 있
다. 26년 3분기 메모리 영업이익만 108.5조원으로 추정한다. 4 분기에는 140조원에 
이를 것으로 전망한다. 
HBM4와 QLC는 차별화 포인트 
삼성전자는 HBM에서 입지가 좋았던 적이 없었다. 점유율, 수익성, 가동률, 제품 성
능, 고객 등 모든 항목에서 경쟁사 대비 열위에 있었다. HBM4는 모든 항목에서 입
지를 개선시킬 변수가 될 것으로 예상한다. 26년 3분기 물량은 경쟁사 수준일 것으
로 보이고 연간 물량도 유사한 수준일 전망이다. 27년에는 가격 인상과 가동률 상승
으로 수익성도 크게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. QLC는 TLC와 함께 AI 모델 진화의 
수혜를 받을 제품으로 빠르게 부상하고 있다. 메타의 Muse 영향이다. 접속자당 가
상 메모리를 NAND에 100GB를 할당하고 있기 때문이다.  
부족한 주주환원으로 투자 우선 순위에서 밀릴 듯 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 
변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, HBM 
시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 
판단하기 때문이다. 목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황
을 고려하면 적정 주가는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치
에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 
제한적일 것으로 판단하기 때문이다.

### Page 2

삼성전자 (005930)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기 매출액은 195.3조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.9% 증가한 195.3조원이다. VD/
가전을 제외한 주요 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 증가했다. DS, MX 모두 물량보다는 
가격 효과가 더 크게 작용한 것으로 분석된다. 사업부별로는  
1) DS 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 15.4% 증가한 147.1조원이다. 메모리
는 26년 2분기 대비 16.4% 증가했다. 26년 3분기 DRAM B/G(Bit Growth)
는 +2.0%, ASP는 +20.0%이다. NAND B/G는 +5.0%, ASP는 +18.0%이다. 
HBM 물량 증가는 HBM4가 주도했고, 그 이하 제품 비중은 하락한 것으로 
추정한다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 7.1% 증가한 8조원이다. 소형
은 해외 고객 및 국내 고객의 신제품 출시 영향으로 ASP 상승, 물량 증가 
효과가 동반되었고, 대형은 게이밍 모니터 비중을 확대했다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 13.4% 증가한 37.7조원이
다. MX는 15.5% 증가했다. 물량은 소폭 감소했고, ASP 상승 영향을 받았다. 
네트워크 사업부 매출액은 2분기 대비 60% 감소했다.  
4) VD/가전 사업부 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 4.1% 감소한 13.9조원이다. VD 
매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 6.5% 감소했다. 가전은 26년 2분기 대비 1.3% 
감소했다.

IBKS RESEARCH │3 
26년 3분기 영업이익은 107.4조원 
삼성전자의 26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 20% 증가한 107.4조원이다. DS,  
디스플레이와 MX는 2분기 대비 증가했고, VD/가전은 2분기 대비 감소했다. 사업부별
로는 
1) DS 사업부는 26년 2분기 대비 19.4% 증가한 106.5조원이다. 2분기 대비 영
업이익률은 소폭 상승했다. 메모리 영업이익은 2분기 대비 19.0% 증가했고, 
비메모리는 영업적자를 유지했다.  
2) 디스플레이 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 54.5% 증가한 1.1조원이다. 
중소형 신제품 효과로 수익성이 개선된 영향이다. 대형도 믹스 개선으로 수
익성이 개선되었다. 
3) MX/네트워크 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자폭이 감소했다. 물량
은 줄었지만 High End 비중 상승으로 ASP는 개선되었고, 원가 비중이 낮아
진 영향이다. 
4) VD/가전 사업부 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 적자 규모가 커졌다.

### Page 6

삼성전자 (005930)

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기 매출액은 226.5조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 16.0% 증가한 226.5조원으로 예상
한다. DS가 증가하고 나머지는 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS는 메모리가 성장을 주도할 것으로 기대한다. 가격과 물량 모두 3분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 전망한다. Context Server에 대한 수요가 증가하고, Personal Agent의 가상메
모리 설정으로 인해 NAND 수요가 증가하면서 QLC에 대한 수요 증가 속도가 빨라질 
전망이다. 이로 인해 NAND Bit Growth는 경쟁사와 차별화된 수준일 것으로 예상한
다.  
HBM 물량도 꾸준히 증가할 것으로 기대한다. 3 분기에도 HBM4를 중심으로 물량이 
크게 증가했다. HBM4는 SK하이닉스와 비슷한 물량일 것으로 예상한다. 아직 전체 
물량은 SK하이닉스가 더 많은 상황이고, HBM 내 HBM4 비중은 업계 최고 수준인 것
으로 판단한다. 26년까지는 HBM이 수익성에는 마이너스 요인이지만, 27년에는 
DRAM 수준의 영업이익이 가능할 것으로 기대한다.  
MX는 비수기 저가 라인 물량 조정으로 전체 물량은 감소하지만, 제품믹스는 개선되
어 판가는 3분기 대비 상승할 것으로 예상한다.

26년 4분기 영업이익은 138.2조원으로 예상 
삼성전자의 26년 4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 28.7% 증가한 138.2조원으로 예
상한다. DS 사업부를 제외하고 모두 3분기 대비 부진할 것으로 예상한다.  
DS 사업부 영업이익은 3분기 대비 30% 증가한 138.5조원으로 전망한다. DRAM, 
NAND 가격이 10% 초중반 수준으로 상승하고, 물량은 10% 중반 이상 증가하면서 3
분기 대비 영업이익률 개선을 기대하기 때문이다. HBM 물량 증가가 이익 개선에 미
치는 영향은 제한적일 것으로 예상한다.  
디스플레이, MX는 물량 부진으로 3분기 대비 영업이익은 부진할 것으로 전망한다.

DS가 성장 주도 
DS만 보자

### Page 7

김운호  6915-5656

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
Check Point 
1. 모자란 듯한 주주환원 
26년 3분기 실적 발표에서 밝힌 주주환원 정책은 지난 8월의 내용과 다르지 않다. 3
분기 배당 30조원(주당 4,601원)을 확정한 정도이다. 잔여 배당 재원이 55~75조원으
로 구체화했고, 현금 배당과 자사주 매입을 병행하겠다고 밝혔다.  
자사주 매입 시 우선주 매입도 고려 대상이라고 언급한 것은 고무적이지만 27년부터 
새롭게 시작되는 주주환원 정책을 결정하기 위한 이사회 개최는 27년 1월에 열릴 것
으로 예고하였기에 연내 추가적인 주주환원 발표는 없을 것으로 판단한다.  
2. HBM4로 HBM 시장에서 입지 강화 
HBM4를 통해서 자신감을 회복하고 있는 것으로 판단한다. 양산 시점은 경쟁사 대비 
빨랐고, 3분기 물량은 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준인 것으로 추정한다. HBM3e가 부진했기 
때문에 전체 생산량에서 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 가장 높은 수준일 것으로 추정한다.  
26년 전체 물량도 경쟁사와 비슷한 수준이 될 것으로 전망한다. 삼성전자는 HBM 시
장 점유율을 DRAM 수준으로 맞추겠다는 목표를 3분기에 달성한 것으로 밝혔다.  
물량이 증가하면서 가동률도 빠르게 개선될 것으로 기대한다. 아직은 DRAM과 영업
이익률에서 큰 차이가 있지만 27년에는 인상된 가격, 높아진 수율, 가동률 개선으로 
DRAM과 영업이익률 차이를 좁힐 수 있을 것으로 기대한다.  
3. QLC NAND 시장 확대 
AI 컴퓨팅 확산은 NAND에게도 성장 기회이다. AI 모델들의 Context Window가 길어
져 KV Cache 메모리에 대한 수요가 커지면서 High Endurance TLC 수요가 급증하고 
있다. DRAM을 대체할 수준의 NAND가 필요하게 되었기 때문이다.  
이 뿐만 아니라 고용량 NAND에 대한 수요도 Personal Agent의 출현으로 급속히 증
가할 것으로 기대한다. 여기에는 QLC NAND가 주로 사용된다. 메타의 Muse는 접속
자 1인에 대해서 DRAM 8GB, NAND 100GB의 가상 메모리를 설정하고 운영한다.  
Agent AI의 확산은 스토리지에 대한 수요도 급격히 확대할 것으로 기대하고, 삼성전
자에게도 기회가 될 것으로 기대한다.  
26년 4분기 NAND Bit Growth는 삼성전자가 가장 높을 것으로 예상한다.

삼성전자 (005930)

8 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 350,000원으로 하향 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 이는 
1) 여러 변수에도 메모리 성장은 변화가 없을 것으로 전망하고, 
2) 메모리 수요는 장기적 성장 국면에 진입했고, 
3) HBM 시장 내 입지가 강화되고 있고, 
4) 현재 주가는 실적 대비 지나치게 저평가된 수준으로 판단하기 때문이다. 
목표주가는 350,000원으로 하향한다. 현재 수익성과 시장 상황을 고려하면 적정 주가
는 460,000원으로 판단하지만 주주환원 정책이 시장 기대치에 미치지 못하고, 경쟁사
와 비교해도 선호도가 낮아질 것을 고려하면 주가 상승은 제한적일 것으로 판단하기 
때문이다.

## Document 238: [012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에_b94e5eb0

Source PDF: all report pdf/[012450] 한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입한화에어로스페이스(012450) 일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에_b94e5eb0.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

한화에어로스페이스 (012450/KS) 
일시적 부진, 4Q부터 성장 가도에 재진입 
2026-10-30

조선/방산. 한승한, RA. 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 1,300,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 760,000 원 
상승여력: 71.1%

Signal: 3Q26, 수출 물량 저조 및 국내 낮은 마진 물량 인식으로 어닝 쇼크 
Key: 4Q부터 내년까지 수출 물량 빠르게 회복하며 실적 성장 이어갈 전망 
Step: 연내 새로운 중장기 성장동력이 될 중동 수주계약 기대. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 수출 물량 납품 저조로 어닝 쇼크  
3Q26 연결 매출액 6 조 943 억원(-6.0% YoY, -34.4% QoQ), 영업이익 4,520억원
(-46.2% YoY, -66.9% QoQ)으로, 시장예상치(1조 1,615억원)를 대폭 하회하는 어닝 쇼
크 기록했다. ①폴란드 중심의 수출 물량의 매출 인식 시점이 4 분기로 이연되며, 수출 물량 
비중이 감소한 점이 컸다. ②내수 물량 또한 감소했으며, 특히 국내 물량 중에서도 상대적
으로 마진율 낮은 유무인 무기체계 초도 물량이 이번 분기에 집중됐다. ③내수&수출 전반
적인 지상방산 매출 비중 감소에 따른 고정비 부담이 컸으며, ④일회성 요인으로 대전 사업
장 사고에 따른 복구 비용 약 300 억원과 충당금 및 기타 비용 총 160 억원, 그리고 환율 
하락으로 영업이익의 약 10% 수준의 감소 효과 발생했다. 다만 4Q26 부터 이번 분기 이
연된 폴란드 중심의 수출 물량 인식이 반영되면서 큰 폭의 탑라인 및 이익 성장 시현할 전
망이며, 대전 사업장 또한 내년 상반기 가동 재개 예상됨에 따라 지상방산 부문은 전년 대비 
성장 폭 두드러지며 사측 가이던스 15% 매출 성장 충분히 달성 가능할 전망이다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 130 만원으로 하향 
한화에어로스페이스에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가 기존 160 만원에서 130
만원으로 하향한다. 최근 Peer 그룹의 주가 하락에 따라 멀티플 하향 조정했기 때문이다. 
폴란드 천무 유도탄 JV에 이어 현지화가 전제된 K9 3차 계약을 추진 중이며, 국내 장약 생
산능력 증설을 통한 유럽향 수요 대응과 미국 내 생산거점 구축까지 진행 중에 있다. 또한 
미국 차륜형 자주포 사업 시제 선정에 이은 궤도형 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다. 중동 국
가들의 방공무기체계에 대한 계약 논의는 매우 가시적인 상황이며, 사우디 MNG 사업 수주 
또한 시점의 문제일 뿐이다. 기존 유럽에 더해 중동은 현지화 전략을 기반으로 동사의 새로
운 중장기 성장동력이 될 지역이며, 올해 4 분기부터 해당 수주 모멘텀과 함께 큰 폭의 실적 
성장이 맞물리며 리레이팅 가능할 전망이다. 선제적 매수 기회라는 판단이다.

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다.

## Document 258: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다LG에너지솔루션(373220) ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-11-04
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026.11.04   
LG에너지솔루션 
(373220)

ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 Company Brief

3Q26 실적, 원-달러 환율 하락에도 시장 기대치 대폭 상회 
동사의 3Q26 실적은 매출 9.6조원(+59% YoY, + 28% QoQ), 영업이익 7,560억원 
(+26% YoY, +568% QoQ)으로 원/달러 환율 하락에도 불구하고 시장기대치(매출 
8.8조원, 영업이익 3,217억원)를 크게 상회했다. 당사는 Stellantis와의 캐나다 
합작관계 종료에 따른 보상금 등을 포함해 약 4,250억원의 일회성 이익이 반영된 
것으로 추정한다. 이를 제외한 영업이익은 약 3,310억원으로, 시장기대치에 대체로 
부합한 것으로 판단된다. AMPC는 미국 ESS 생산량 증가와 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동에 
힘입어 전분기 대비 약 73% 증가한 4,169억원을 기록했다. 사업부문별로는 ESS의 
실적 개선이 두드러졌다. 상반기 배터리 패키징 병목으로 이연됐던 물량 출하가 
집중되면서 ESS 출하량은 전분기 대비 60% 가량 증가한 것으로 추정된다. 자동차 
전지도 유럽향 물량 회복과 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동으로 매출이 전분기 대비 약 
33%로 증가했으며, 고정비 부담 완화에 따라 적자폭도 뚜렷하게 축소된 것으로 
판단된다. 소형전지는 주요 고객사인 Tesla의 판매 호조에 따른 출하 증가로 매출 
회복과 수익성을 뒷받침한 것으로 보인다. 
4Q26 ESS 중심의 수익성 개선과 수주 모멘텀 지속될 전망 
미국 ESS 시장은 신재생 에너지 확대와 AI 데이터센터의 전력 수요 증가에 힘입어 
빠르게 성장하고 있다. 특히 정책 환경도 비PFE 공급망을 확보한 업체에 유리하게 
작용하고 있다. 동사는 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스 콜에서 ESS 수주잔고가 약 
160GWh이며, 2028 년까지 캐파의 대부분에 대응하는 물량을 확보했다고 밝혔다. 
또한 현재 다수의 고객사들과 2028년 이후 공급 물량을 협의하고 있어 연말까지 
추가적인 수주 모멘텀이 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이를 바탕으로 4Q26에도 동사의 
본업 실적은 ESS 중심의 개선세를 이어갈 전망이다. 전분기에 반영된 대규모 일회성 
이익의 기저효과로 영업이익은 감소하겠지만, 이를 제외하면 출하량 증가와 가동률 
상승에 따른 점진적인 수익성 개선이 예상된다. 수익성 중심의 사업 효율화를 위해 
소형 IT용 파우치 사업을 축소하고 있다는 점도 중장기 손익 개선에 긍정적이다. 이를 
반영한 4Q26 매출과 영업이익은 각각 약 10.5조원(+63% YoY, +9% QoQ), 
4,150억원(흑자전환 YoY, -45% QoQ)으로 추정된다. 
매수 투자의견과 목표주가 유지 
동사에 대한 매수 투자의견과 목표주가를 유지한다. 북미 ESS 수요 증가와 공급망 
재편에 따른 신규 수주 확대가 중장기 실적 성장을 뒷받침할 전망이다. 특히 ESS용 
생산라인 전환과 가동률 상승이 본격화되면 매출 증가에 더해 고정비 부담 완화와 
AMPC 수혜 확대가 맞물리면서 수익성도 개선될 것으로 예상된다. ESS 의 역할이 
신재생 에너지 연계용 저장장치를 넘어 AI 데이터센터의 안정적인 전력 공급을 
지원하는 핵심 인프라로 확대되고 있다는 점에도 주목할 필요가 있다. 이에 따라 
동사의 밸류에이션 역시 기존 EV 중심의 평가에서 벗어나 전력 인프라 산업의 
성장성을 반영하는 방향으로 재평가되어야 한다. 업종 내 실적 개선 가시성과 정책 
수혜가 뚜렷하고, 4Q26 신규 수주에 따른 주가 상승 가능성도 높다는 점에서 동사에 
대한 비중 확대 전략이 유효하다는 판단이다.

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.

 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상

 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
We reaffirm our expectation  that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board will again increase the cash rate at the November
meeting. Today’s inflation data and Senate testimony are
the final pieces of information before the RBA Monetary
Policy Board (MPB) meets next week. Recent data and

communication have consistently pointed to the staff
recommending an increase in the cash rate. We expect a
majority of the external members to agree, though some
members might dissent.
Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions. It

will therefore deem that further policy tightening is needed.
The RBA has made it clear that it is down-weighting the recent
easing in the labour market, even though this was more than it
forecast. In today’s opening statement  to the Senate hearing,
the Governor said, “Despite the unemployment rate recently
rising a little more than we had expected, the labour market
seems a little tighter than we had previously judged. The
evidence for this is elevated underlying inflationary pressures.

That is, when we exclude some of the large volatile price
movements such as fuel we see inflationary pressure in areas
that reflect domestic demand for goods and services.” In the
Senate, the Governor also said that they were not worried
about the current pace of increase in unemployment.
Of course, this takes no account of the lagged relationship
between the labour market and inflation. The argument is that
if that measure of inflation last quarter was too high, it must

therefore be that the labour market is too tight. Nor does it
allow sufficiently for non-labour cost shocks, which business
surveys indicate are the real issue. But whether this judgement
by the RBA turns out to be right or not will not be revealed
for some time, certainly not soon enough to change its mind
before next week. And while some MPB members might have
a more optimistic view about labour supply trends than the
RBA staff, they are likely to be a minority.

The RBA is also relatively sanguine about the current
downturn in the housing market. The Governor and other
RBA officials are at pains to highlight that they do not target
housing prices; nor should they. The RBA considers that most,
though not all, of the current downswing is an expected effect
of tighter monetary policy.
RBA to hike in November, as inflation remains too high
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The question then turns to the future beyond November. While

we still expect a hawkish tone in the RBA’s communication
until inflation has clearly turned down, further hikes beyond
next week are not currently our base case. Clearly, though, the
risks of another hike – most likely in February – have increased.
Our current forecast for Q4 inflation would seem to give them
cover for such a move. We note, however, that home-building
costs in the CPI have undershot expectations in the past couple
of months. This might imply a different view of the extent of

domestic demand pressures than the RBA currently believes.
However, a lot can happen in three months, as we have just
seen. The conflict in the Middle East  could escalate further,
lifting energy prices beyond our current base case. It could
also go the other way, though, if the US and Iran manage to
strike a post-midterm-election deal. And as their counterparts
in New Zealand highlighted today, one risk is that the global
economy weakens suddenly. In the post-meeting statement ,

the RBNZ Monetary Policy Committee said, “the world
economy could tip abruptly into a downturn causing weaker
inflation over the medium term.” This risk was something the
RBNZ MPC thought could crystallise in the next six weeks.
Whether on this timetable or something more drawn out, this
possibility does not appear to be on the RBA’s radar. Nor is it
our base case, though we have previously worried that the AI
financing boom could hit a wall quite suddenly.

“We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA
Monetary Policy Board will again increase the
cash rate at the November meeting"

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related
risks through 2027; although, in the absence of another shock,
we expect them to remain on hold.
The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view

on the breadth of growth, the 3.4% annualised gain for GDP
benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household
consumption, 12.7% growth in non-residential investment
as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment
was effectively offset by net exports’ drag given most of
the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is
imported. This is not to say that US firms won’t benefit from

the investment once complete, but simply that much of the
initial return goes to the foreign businesses who provide the
necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks
remain for the consumer, the increase in nominal spending
seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis,
consumption flat in the month following a 0.5% gain in
August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the
outlook is expected to see household consumption growth

average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE
inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr medium-term target as
the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline
and core measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy
rates unchanged in October. Of note from the meeting
communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks

for inflation, but also downside risks for growth. "While the
full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying
inflation has remained broadly stable so far and wages do not
show a material response". "Financing conditions have [also]
tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth".
In the press conference, President Lagarde noted that,
between the October and December meetings, a significant
amount of new economic information will be released,

allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.
Based on the October meeting communications, we continue
to expect a rate hike at the December meeting but believe this
will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the
spectrum of risks Europe faces is broad and skewed towards
inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that
she will step down as ECB President in February ahead of the

scheduled conclusion of her term next October.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI .
Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in the month, largely
owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in
domestic and international holiday travel & accommodation.
The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than
anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).
While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation –
housing in particular looks to be cooling faster than expected

as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are
still showing persistence. Highlighting this is market services
ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator
of price pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this
metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail points
to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for
another chunky read in Q4.
With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts,

and some of the energy risks flagged then materialising, we
continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp
rate hike to 4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate
hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market
expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding instead to wait for
further information before taking the next step with policy. The
decision was made 4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A

25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening in 2027
(Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems
probable; however, each decision will depend on fuel prices,
inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady  at its October
meeting. Based on prior comments by key members, the
decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook

and Cleveland Fed President Hammack’s dissents in favour
of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at
a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Chair
Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic
growth outside of tech-related investment (evinced by Q3
GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in
the statement. However, inflation “remains elevated” and the

FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable
return of annual inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will
help the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests
another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter, the FOMC
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
A lesson to be learned
The RBNZ delivered the biggest shock to markets outside of
a crisis period in over 20 years this week by leaving the OCR
unchanged. Markets had priced around a 98% probability of
a 25bp increase, and all major domestic analysts concurred.

After the CPI data the week earlier, we belatedly shifted our
call to a hike despite plenty of good arguments in favour of
the RBNZ sticking to its plan to pause. With the benefit of
hindsight, too much weight was put on market pricing and
short-term inflation drivers and not enough on the medium
term. That’s an important lesson to be learned.
The highlight of an action-packed week of central banking news
was the unexpected decision of the RBNZ to leave the OCR at

2.75%, despite near unanimous analyst expectations and market
pricing in favour of a 25bp hike. We had shifted sides from a
hold to a hike just a week earlier, in the wake of a CPI outcome
that was weaker than RBNZ expectations on the surface, but
with some evidence of firm core inflation undertones.
While we clearly cannot claim to have gotten this call right,
we always thought that the outcome was not the “done deal”
that markets had priced in. For example, in our preview of the

meeting we noted:
“We still think a more measured tightening approach is
appropriate from here. Raising the OCR at every meeting
until something ‘breaks’ could be a risky strategy if downside
growth concerns crystalise.”
And we saw a vote on the decision as very possible:
“A vote seems very possible as differences in view will likely
remain among the MPC about how much the medium-term
inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.”
The 4:3 vote that was ultimately required to bring the MPC

to a decision is a testament to just how finely balanced the
decision was. We had thought that the hike was perhaps
around a 60-70% probability whereas the Record of Meeting
implies something more like a 50:50 call.
In the final analysis, the RBNZ has merely followed through on
the pause it signalled for this meeting back in the September
Monetary Policy Statement. All MPC members continue to see
a case for a higher OCR in time, but there are different views

around the timing of those increases. Governor Breman noted
that further interest rate increases remain likely in coming
meetings but are conditional on the whether the outlook
evolves in line with expectations. Indeed, the Governor noted
in later comments to press that a December hike was a “high”
probability and that the outlook remains for OCR “hikes” as the
base case. This suggests that the RBNZ’s view on the terminal
OCR hasn’t really shifted down from the 3.25% level presented

back in September. And it may be reasonable to speculate,
subject to data evolving as the RBNZ expects, that a modest
upgrade in those forward OCR forecasts could be in the offing.
So how did we all get this so wrong? This outcome was a real
shock given the historical record shows that over the history of
the OCR the market has without exception correctly predicted
a 25bp hike when the implied probability built into markets
was as high as it was. Clearly some recalibration of the model

is required. We can identify two key areas where lessons
should be learned: too much weight on market pricing; and not
enough on drivers of medium-term inflation.
Market pricing did not adequately reflect the
MPC’s desire to shift strategy .
Firstly, and most importantly, we put too much weight on
market pricing and too little on the RBNZ’s clearly stated
strategy of preferring a pause at the October meeting to
assess the impact of interest rate increases already done. In our

Preview we incorrectly surmised that silence equated to assent
and that market pricing of a hike would make an OCR hike the
less disruptive option – especially given the sense that a hike
to 3% was still likely in December as implied by the RBNZ’s
September forecasts. From a macroeconomic perspective,
raising rates six weeks early is of little consequence.
This logic, as sound as it was, was wrong, as market pricing did
not reflect a large enough change in the medium-term inflation

outlook to justify a change in RBNZ strategy. Markets at least
partially held the view, based on a long history of revealed
RBNZ preference, that the RBNZ keeps moving the OCR in the
same direction until confronted with compelling evidence that
a halt to the cycle is in prospect. That operating approach may
be under review now.
It’s not uncommon for other central banks to take breaks
while amid an interest rate cycle to gather information on
the impact of work already done before moving forward.

This week’s pause by the FOMC in the face of a fierce set of
monthly inflation readings in the last month is an example.
Pausing does not need to imply stopping or reversing. Indeed,
taking a more measured approach could prove to be solid
risk management – as we identified in our MPR preview note.
Risk management concerns probably should have greater
prominence in this cycle given we are seeing a measure of
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
supply side cost shocks that might not be persistent. The
Statement of Record contains a wealth of material related to
these sorts of uncertainties and concerns. Markets were not
listening to these concerns, however.
An insufficient focus on drivers of medium-term

inflation
Markets were not listening to the alarm bells because there
was a heavy focus on factors that may be more relevant to the
short-term inflation outlook than medium-term inflation. Top
of mind were the rise in energy prices and fall in the exchange
rate. Both factors look set to significantly boost the short-
term inflation outlook – for example we currently forecast
December quarter annual inflation of 4.3%, with upside risks
from there if fuel prices do not fall over the balance of the

quarter. However, our analysis of the data surprises suggested
no significant shift to the implied RBNZ OCR track one year
ahead. Upward pressure on the future OCR projection from
the lower exchange rate and stronger GDP was balanced
by higher long term interest rates and the lower Q3 CPI
outcome. Higher energy prices were not seen as a key factor
as they don’t impact medium term inflation unless inflation
expectations are dislodged (no evidence was found of that

since September) or if the increase in energy prices is viewed
as persistent (futures markets continue to imply lower forward
energy prices, although MPC hawks clearly see risks of
persistent inflation coming from supply shocks).
The RBNZ’s communications at the MPR and in its aftermath
are directing markets to pay more attention to medium-term
inflation drivers in the future. Hence the references to the
strength of demand and inflation expectations. The risk to

the RBNZ’s inflation forecasts are to the upside, and hence
we might reasonably expect some upgrade of the OCR profile
when we see new forecasts in December. But it’s not a done
deal by any means as data and events between now and then
will dictate what stance the RBNZ takes. This week’s business
and consumer confidence reports do not seem to present many
smoking guns of imminent second-round inflation pressures,
even though business and consumer sentiment seems to be

taking the higher oil price in its stride.
Is core inflation high enough yet to shift the
strategy?
Another interesting point made by the Governor was that the
markets (and analysts) misinterpreted how the September
quarter CPI was read by the RBNZ, which evidently took
greater comfort from the outcome. Governor Breman has noted
she wished the RBNZ has protocols in place to communicate
with markets the RBNZ’s view on that CPI data given that it
seems to have been a factor that figured importantly in at least

some MPC member’s thinking. Headline inflation was lower
than the RBNZ expected and importantly the core inflation
measures, that we had thought might have been of concern to
the MPC, do not yet seem to have been at a level sufficient to
shift the RBNZ from its pause-then-hike strategy. We remain
sceptical that a 3.25% OCR will prove sufficient to bring core
inflation down significantly. But core inflation appears to need
to lift for the MPC to become more agitated. It likely will lift,

but we won’t get more data on that until January 2027.
December hike likely but not a done deal
We expect a further 25bp hike at this year’s final meeting on 9
December and then 25bp increases in the February and March
2027 meetings, followed by a slower pace of increases at the
June and September Monetary Policy Statement meetings.
Our forecast of a peak OCR of 4% from September 2027 has
remained unchanged throughout, as has our assessment of a
3.75% neutral OCR.

That said, in the current uncertain environment, it goes without
saying that the evolution of monetary policy will depend on
how both global events and key data evolve. Therefore, as the
RBNZ noted itself, the exact timing of the tightening profile
is highly uncertain and even the tightening we forecast at the
December meeting should not be regarded as a done deal.
Looking ahead, the ‘hardest’ data to come will be the Q3
labour market surveys where we anticipate a small fall in

the unemployment rate to 5.5% but still moderate wage
pressures. The December quarter RBNZ inflation expectations
surveys will be of significant interest (17, 20 and 24
November) for MPC members looking for persistent inflation
impacts. The usual run of monthly activity and selected prices
indicators will be useful in narrowing down Q4 growth and
inflation momentum. And finally, the MPC’s focus on external
risks will imply keeping an eye on global fixed income, equity

and credit markets for signs of imminent calamity.
Finally, November 7 is the general election. A close race is
envisaged that could impact on the RBNZ’s mandate. We
will be offering client briefings on Monday 9 November and
Tuesday 10 November to outline the immediate implications
of the election outcome for markets and the economy. Contact
your relationship manager for details.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: RBA Policy Decision (%)
Nov 3, Last: 4.60, Westpac f/c: 4.85
Market f/c: 4.85, Range: 4.60 to 4.85
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) will decide to raise the cash rate by 25bps to
4.85% at the November meeting.

Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions.
Recent data and communication have consistently pointed to
the RBA staff recommending an increase in the cash rate. We
expect a majority of the external board members to agree,

though some members might dissent.
AUS: Oct Cotality Home Value Index (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –1.1, Westpac f/c: –1.3
The Cotality home value index – covering all of Australia – fell
1.1% in Sep with downward revisions shaving a further 0.4%
off prices. The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2%
from their March peak, annual growth stalling flat. Cotality
reported 97% of capital city suburbs experienced falls over the
three months to September.
The October update is set to be another weak one with an

added hit coming from the RBA’s interest rate rise in late
September. The daily measure (covering the five major capital
cities rather than all of Australia) is tracking a 1.5% decline vs
1.3% in September. The detail points to slightly bigger falls
in Sydney, Brisbane and Perth with Melbourne continuing
to record more moderate declines. Note that seasonality is
usually a slight positive in the October month.
AUS: Sep Dwelling Approvals (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –6.1, Westpac f/c: –7.0

Market f/c: 2.0, Range: –7.0 to 5.0
Dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the annual
pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. Weakness centred on unit
approvals with private detached house approvals actually
posting a solid rebound from a July month fall, touching a new
cycle high (up 18.4%yr).
We expect the September update to show a much clearer
weakening. New construction is coming under pressure
from multiple fronts: interest rate rises; a sharp downturn in

established markets; and rising costs – with a great deal of
uncertainty around each of these. New home sales have fell
sharply through the September quarter and we expect to
see detached house approvals show something similar. Unit
approvals are coming off a weak read and should be more
stable. Total approvals are expected to print 7% lower.

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Goods Trade Balance ($bn)
Nov 5, Last: 0.5, Westpac f/c: 2.0
Market f/c: 1.0, Range: –1.0 to 2.5
Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August.
Recently, the trade balance has been oscillating between
small deficits and surpluses with significant volatility in the

underlying trade mix.
Swings in non-monetary gold, fuel and cars have all played
a large role, but the most eye-catching result from Aug was
the 79% surge in automatic data processing equipment (ADP)
imports, which captures the investment upswing related to
data centres.
For September, we expect an increase in the surplus as the
ADP import surge unwinds, though we are likely in store for
ongoing monthly volatility in trade for some time.
NZ: Sep Building Consents (%mth)

Nov 3, Last: +5.6%, Westpac f/c: Flat
Annual residential building consent numbers are currently at
a three year high, and we expect that they will remain firm in
the September update. However, monthly consent numbers
are showing signs of peaking, with falls in three of the last four
months. We’re watching to see if that trend has continued,
particularly given the ongoing softness in the housing
market.  In the non-residential space, we’ve seen softness

in the number of retail and office developments, but greater
resilience in the industrial and storage segment.
AUS: Sep Household Spending Indicator (%mth)
Nov 4, Last: flat, Westpac f/c: –0.5
Market f/c: 0.2, Range: –0.5 to 0.8
The household spending indicator was unchanged in August,
annual growth easing to 6.8%yr. The detail showed a modest
rotation away from discretionary categories towards non-
discretionary.
Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker  showed a decent rise in

the September month although new vehicle sales (which are
not covered by the tracker but are in scope for the spending
indicator) look to have posted a decline. Overall we expect
the indicator to show a 0.5% decline in the September month.
Note that the update with also include Q3 estimates of real
spending – we expect this to show a 0.6%qtr gain with prices
up 1.2%qtr and nominal spend up 1.8%qtr.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 Labour Market Surveys (%)
Nov 4, Unemployment Rate, Last: 5.6, Westpac f/c: 5.5
LCI Private Sector, Last: 0.7, Westpac f/c: 0.5
We expect the unemployment rate to edge down to 5.5%
for the September quarter. Job numbers have been gaining
momentum in recent months, and now look to be enough to

outstrip population growth. However, the existing slack in the
labour market is likely to be keeping wage growth subdued,
despite cost-of-living pressures on workers. Our forecasts are
a little more positive overall than what the RBNZ assumed in
its September MPS forecasts.
US: Oct Employment Report
Nov 6, Payrolls, Last: 29k, Market f/c: 55k, WBC f/c: 75k
Nov 6, U/E rate, Last: 4.2%, Market f/c: 4.2%, WBC f/c: 4.3%
Nonfarm payrolls have shown considerable volatility of late,

July’s -10k followed by a 133k gain in August then a 29k
increase in September. However, the 3-month average of 51k
is likely a good guide on underlying momentum. In October, we
look for a 75k gain, although we expect some of that increase
to be offset by a modest reduction to the prior two months,
keeping the 3-month average around 50k.
While the participation rate has lifted in recent months, it
remains well below the average of 2025. Month-to-month, a

change either way is not guaranteed, but it seems likely that
the participation rate will trend higher in coming months. We
therefore expect the unemployment rate to edge up.

## Document 293: Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms

Source PDF: all report pdf/Blue Wave_ Democrats sweep the US midterms.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-11-05
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Blue Wave: Democrats 
sweep the US midterms  
Summary 
Event: The Democrats gained control of the House of 
Representatives and the Senate at the US midterm elections 
that took place this week.  
Our View: With the US government now no longer under 
unified control of the Republicans, gridlock has returned to 
Washington. We believe investors should not overreact to 
these results and should stay invested. 
In equities, investors should  continue to diversify their 
equity exposure into more defensive sectors.  As we 
transition to mid -cycle for the equity markets , we expect 
stocks to continue to deliver positive returns in the 8 -10% 
range per annum, a downshift from the torrid pace of gains in 
the earlier part of the cycle.  
In bonds, income remains attractive given recent spread 
widening. We favour non -hyperscaler investment grade 
corporate bonds at the three to seven -year part of the 
curve. We continue to expect the US dollar to remain range-
bound in the near  term, as we expect two more rate hikes 
from the Fed, but as this mini rate hike cycle ends in the 
second quarter of 2027, the USD index (DXY) is likely to 
depreciate towards 98.5 as markets start to anticipate a Fed 
rate cut in the second half of next year.  
Oil is likely to rebound in the short -term on heightened 
geopolitical risk, but as supply chains adapt to the current 
situation and Congress exercises oversight on defense 
spending, WTI prices are likely to drift towards our 12-month 
target of USD 80 per barrel.

Democrats made solid gains across the Senate, House 
of Representatives and State Governors 
Results reported as at 7:45PM Singapore Time

Source: The Wall Street Journal, Standard Chartered

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 5 November 2026

Major implications of Democrat control of 
Congress   
1) Fiscal expansionary policies may slow. Democrats are 
now in position to block any new tax cuts or spending plans 
from the Trump administration. At the same time, any fiscal 
policies proposed by Democrats may also face Trump’s veto. 
Hence the fiscal trajectory of the US will likely remain stuck  
over the next two years. As we get into the second half of 
2027, the US debt ceiling will once again loom, and gridlock 
could potentially bring back government shutdowns. 
However, as they have always done in the past, compromise 
will ultimately prevail, in our opinion.  
2) Trump remains in control of foreign policy.  With the 
midterms now out of the way, could Trump decide to re-start 
combat operations with Iran? We believe there is a risk that 
US and Israel – now that both countries’ have concluded 
important elections – will once again be tempted to restart 
combat operations to force an end to the conflict. However, 
the duration of any renewed hostilities may be limited by 
Congress next year as the Democrats will likely withhold 
funding for further military operations. In the near -term, oil 
prices may experience renewed volatility before stabilising at 
a lower level next year. The introduction of new tariffs on 
trading partners may prove harder with Democrat oversight. 
3) AI data centre moratoriums could grow, though model 
development regulatory changes would be harder to 
pass. While the headlines have focused on the House and 
Senate results, one important development has also 
occurred at the gubernatorial level. Notably, Democrats have 
won the governor races in Texas and Georgia, two big data 
centre states. As data centre build out permits are largely 
decided at the local state level, this will have important 
ramifications for build out plans next year.  However, any 
Democratic proposals to regulate AI model developments 
would need to be signed off by President Trump, who 
continues to wield veto power on any such proposals.   
Asset class strategy 
Equities: Remain invested and continue to diversify .  
While investors initially overreacted, President Trump’s 
relatively conciliatory response has provided some welcome 
relief, helping markets recover strongly from their pre-market 
lows. Hence, we recommend investors avoid 
overreacting, remain invested and continue to diversify 
their equity exposure across sectors and regions.  
A few themes stand out from the early pre-market reaction to 
the mid-term elections. On the positive side, defensive stocks 
continue to fare well, while themes favoured by Democrats 
are also outperforming. Clean energy is a notable 
beneficiary, with solar s tocks among the biggest winners

Policy uncertainty and a change in the innovation cycle 
is consistent with our view of a mid-cycle transition, 
where equity returns moderate but remain positive

today. Affordability-related themes are also rallying, including

Medicaid-related stocks are also moving higher on

On the negative side, semiconductor weakness continues

Our AI capex estimates remain conservative relative to 
consensus, but with a potential reset in expectations around

After downgrading semiconductors last month, we have 
maintained a conservative stance on the industry, with a 
lower recommended allocation compared to September. 
While we believe the mid -term election outcome is not a 
complete surprise to markets, particularly given pre -election 
polling and the recent weakness in both the S&P 500 and 
Nasdaq, the overnight correction highlights how complacent 
investors had become, with the VIX initially surging more than 
10% in pre -market trading, although down from intra -day 
highs after President Trump’s comments. 
As we have highlighted in our recent notes on the transition 
to mid-cycle, this phase is typically characterised by greater 
political headwinds . T he mid -term election  outcome 
reinforces the trend. The experience of 2015 -2017, 
highlighted in the second table, offers a useful historical 
parallel. Despite the knee-jerk reaction, investors should not 
overreact based on one day’s moves. While we had trimmed 
equities to a core allocation last week, we continue to expect 
equities to deliver mid-to-high single-digit returns per annum 
during the mid -cycle, even amid political headwinds, 
supported by steady underlying earnings growth in both 2027 
and 2028. 
So, what should investors do to navigate the next few months 
before the newly elected representatives are sworn in and 
take control of the House and Senate?  As we have been 
highlighting in recent notes, equity markets should still deliver 
positive returns, albeit at a more moderate pace. Hence, we 
recommend staying invested but diversification remains key  
to navigating the next phase of the cycle.  
FX: DXY near-term support at 100.7, 12 -month target at 
98.5. A Democratic sweep potentially removes the legislative 
runway for further tariff escalation and for fiscal expansion, 
the two structural pillars of US exceptionalism that have 
anchored dollar demand in 2026. Reduced fiscal impulse 
compresses the growth and yield differential that has kept the 
US dollar well-bid all year. Additionally, potential Democratic 
restrictions on AI and data centre investment could weigh on 
US equity outperformance, which is likely to act as a 
secondary dollar-negative channel.

DXY headwind from a Democratic sweep, the magnitude will

Fixed Income: Still attractive all -in yields ; prefer 
investment grade corporates at three -to-seven year  
tenors to earn income and manage interest rate risk. The 
US Treasury bond market reaction appears to reflect a 
moderation in expectations of future economic growth after 
the election results. Yields at the intermediate end  of the 
curve dropped the most, while the 30 -year US yield 
moderated more modestly. We continue to expect two more 
Fed rate hikes between now and H1 2027 as still sticky 
inflation and the robust economy support further tightening.  
In the past few weeks, the US Treasury yield curve has 
broadly shifted downwards, after our opportunistic call to go 
long 10-year Treasuries at the end of September. We are of 
the view the ultra-long end downshift in yields is overdone 
(given the still challenging US fiscal trajectory) and would 
recommend investors who had earlier taken long duration 
exposures at the 20 to 30-year parts of the curve reduce their 
positions now. We continue to believe the 3 to 7-year part of 
the curve offers the most optimal balance of income and 
interest rate risk, particularly via investment corporate bonds. 
Within corporate credit, we expect a potentially slower pace 
of AI data centre buildouts could marginally improve market 
technicals, as hyperscaler and tech sector bond issuance 
may slow  (and reduce “crowding out” of other bond 
issuances). Conversely, from a credit perspective, we believe 
this dynamic may also make the data centre focused deals 
less viable, and  would be cautious on some of this sector’s 
lower quality names. Meanwhile, less AI capex dependent 
issuers continue to offer solid credit profiles amidst the still 
strong economy. High yield corporate spreads have widened 
in recent weeks but dispersion wi thin the complex is wide, 
with BBs and Bs credit spreads staying relatively resilient, 
while CCCs have widened to distressed levels. For income -
focused investors, we would recommend staying within the 
crossover range of between BBB-BB.  
Commodities: As noted in the opening section, we believe 
there is a higher near -term risk of a restart of combat 
operations in Iran given the conclusion of the midterm 
elections. Given that, we believe there is a risk that oil 
prices could see a short -term spike , despite recent 
diplomatic overtures from Iran. However, for 2027 we 
continue to expect oil to stabilise at lower levels , as we 
think the Mideast situation should de -escalate given 
Democratic oversight of the defense budget, and the likely re-
configuration of oil supply chains. On the margin, we are also 
closely monitoring regulatory developments in the US shale

We expect oil prices to drift towards USD 80 a barrel as 
oil supply chains adapt, and Congress restrains the US

## Document 306: Carsten’s view of 2027

Source PDF: all report pdf/Carsten’s view of 2027.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Underneath a thick layer of risk and fear, 2027 could turn out
to be a surprisingly benign year for the global economy. At
least if politics, (AI) investment, stock markets and central
banks fall into place at the right time
Carsten Brzeski
As we head into 2027, power plays are intensifying on all fronts.
American voters have just had their say in the midterm elections,
and a president who has spent two years reshaping the post-war
international order now faces a Congress with the power to push

back. In Europe, too, a crowded election calendar will put
governments and political alliances to the test. And beyond the
ballot box, a diﬀerent kind of power struggle is playing out: the
battle for control of trade, technology and energy. Put it all
together, and the outlook starts to read like a high-stakes political
thriller.
For two years, one current has dominated the global economy:
artiﬁcial intelligence. A handful of US tech giants have poured

hundreds of billions of dollars into data centres, chips and models,

### Page 2

propping up US growth and carrying equity markets to record
highs. In 2027, that surge is likely to ease somewhat. That by itself
is not a cause for alarm. The current is spreading into grids, power
plants and infrastructure, and into the many ﬁrms that are only
now beginning to put AI to work. Growth should become less
spectacular, but broader. How stock markets respond is for investors
to decide.
An important prerequisite for the AI boom to end in a soft landing is

falling energy prices. If they do fall, central banks might ﬁnally be
able to do something they have rarely done since the pandemic: sit
on the sidelines. If prices remain high, however, the picture changes.
Central banks could become the circuit breaker, forced to raise rates
more aggressively to contain inﬂation, at the risk of breaking
something along the way. And it might not be the tech sector that
breaks. Instead, policymakers could ﬁnd themselves confronting an

uncomfortable trade-oﬀ: how much pain are they willing to inﬂict
to contain the inﬂationary pressures of an AI-fuelled boom, both in
the real economy and ﬁnancial markets? Tighten too hard, and the
global economy could be in for a much rougher ride. Remember,
some of history’s biggest stock market crashes have been preceded
by sharp monetary tightening.
The AI race has opened another, quite literal, power play.
Households and traditional industry are increasingly competing

with AI and data centres for energy, and it is those more traditional
parts of the economy that look far more vulnerable to higher
interest rates. Even if energy prices fall as tensions in the Middle
East ease, Europe still faces a structural disadvantage: energy
remains more expensive than for many of its competitors. This
problem still needs to be addressed.
And then there’s politics. The impact of the US midterms will clearly
spill over into the new year. The question is whether Donald Trump

becomes a conventional ‘lame duck’, or an altogether less
predictable ‘angry duck’. Early statements from Trump and senior
Republicans immediately after the midterm results suggested his
ﬁnal two years in oﬃce could be conciliatory. The appointment of
